21: # Citation Discipline — Cross-Cutting Doctrine
22: # Reference: Z:/repos/deps/autoresearch/.claude/skills/autoresearch/SKILL.md (verified 2026-04-18 in parent; deps-shared with sss)
23: # Reference: Z:/claude/.claude/projects/Z--claude/memory/feedback_reference_first_hard_rule.md (parent hard rule, 2026-04-18 — PARENT-ATTRIBUTION; sss memory port pending)
24: # Retracted: ARIS + QWED-AI + lean-ctx anti-pattern refs — none of those repos exist in Z:/repos/deps/
25: 
26: ## Rule: Every new pattern MUST cite its source at file:line depth
27: 
28: 1. **Header comment**: `# Reference: Z:/repos/deps/<repo>/<file>:<line>` or `# Reference: <official-docs-URL>`
29: 2. **Never cite READMEs alone** — cite the actual source code implementation
30: 3. **Star count does NOT indicate quality** — read the source, verify the claim
31: 4. **Verify before trust** — the cited file must still exist AND still say what you cite (labels decay)
32: 5. **Silent fallback = violation** — if expected output doesn't appear (e.g., `embeddings: 0` after `--embeddings`), that's a STOP, not a footnote (parent `feedback_reference_first_hard_rule.md` — PARENT-ATTRIBUTION pending sss memory port)
33: 6. **TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE class for inherited patterns** — when a SOTA pattern is derived from a named author's published work (book / peer-reviewed paper / conference talk), cite verbatim-quote + author-name + publication inline. The named-author cite is TIER-1 primary-source for the PATTERN (not the implementing repo); use alongside repo file:line cites for full provenance. Exemplar: `Z:/repos/deps/mattpocock/skills/README.md` (48,857★, MIT, [VERIFIED 2026-04-30 PM via curl raw GitHub]) quotes Pragmatic Programmer (Thomas & Hunt) for "rate of feedback is your speed limit" (failure-mode #3 framing), Domain-Driven-Design (Eric Evans) for ubiquitous-language pattern (#2 framing), and Extreme Programming Explained (Kent Beck) for "invest in design every day" (#4 framing). Per `.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota/memory/reference_mattpocock_skills_pattern_extract_2026_04_30.md` for the full mining trace + adoption-candidate ranking. **TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE PROVISIONAL-EXCEPTION** *(round-3 framing per T3 round-2 conf=0.88 ESCALATING finding on commit `d4df352`; supersedes round-1 "full cardinal-rule-1 satisfaction" framing — physical-only sources do NOT fully satisfy committed-tree inspectability)*: physical-only printed-book/journal/conference-publication primary anchor (ISBN + page + verbatim-quote) is a PROVISIONAL-EXCEPTION to cardinal-rule-1 (CLAUDE.md L7), NOT full satisfaction. Cite class downgraded to TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL with REQUIRED metadata: (a) ISBN/DOI + page + verbatim-quote inline; (b) verifier-identity disclosure (who-verified-when, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-04 by orchestrator against book Tip #15 verbatim text]`); (c) explicit `non-automatable: true` marker so review hooks know to defer to human sign-off; (d) **HARD-GATE ELIGIBILITY TEST** *(Round-3 amendment a per T3 R3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 same-class finding on commit `1b87a6e`; supersedes round-2 "MUST prefer" soft preference framing — preference is not gateable; eligibility is)*: PROVISIONAL admission is admissible ONLY when DOI landing page + accessible PDF / publisher excerpt / archived scan (license-permitting) was SEARCHED AND UNAVAILABLE. The commit body MUST document the search (which DOI/publisher/archive was queried + return shape — e.g., "searched ACM DOI 10.1145/<doi> 2026-05-05 — paywall-only, no excerpt found; searched Internet Archive — no scan; searched publisher excerpt page — none"). Mere preference is INSUFFICIENT — if any auditable surrogate exists (DOI/PDF/excerpt/scan), use it as the normative source instead of the physical-only book cite. e.g., ACM DOI `https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/<doi>` for ACM papers IS auditable when paywall-free. Secondary aggregator URLs (Wikipedia, `https://pragprog.com/tips/`, `https://martinfowler.com/bliki/<topic>.html`, `c2.com/cgi/wiki`) are VERIFICATION PROXIES only — they help re-verify the primary quote without owning the book physically, but are NOT cited as normative source. **Independent named-reviewer sign-off mandate** *(Round-3 amendment b per T3 R3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 same-class finding on commit `1b87a6e`; supersedes round-2 "Lower-confidence reviewer-sign-off mandate" sole-orchestrator framing — sole-orchestrator verification is no longer admissible)*: rule edits citing TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL MUST cite a SECOND verifier-identity BEYOND the orchestrator who replayed the verbatim quote against the source — commit body MUST embed BOTH verifier-identity-1 (the orchestrator, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-05 by orchestrator against book Tip #15 verbatim text]`) AND verifier-identity-2 (the independent named reviewer, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-05 by <human-name> against same book + same page + same quote]`) with explicit `[VERIFIED yyyy-mm-dd by <name> against <source>]` markers for each. The 2-verifier requirement closes the "self-attestation only" gap that round-2 left open. **`non-automatable: true` operator-discipline mandate (manual-only; no hook enforcement YET)** *(Round-4 amendment per T3 round-4 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 SAME-CLASS finding on commit `d07ac71`; supersedes Round-3 amendment c "BLOCKING in T3/T4/T6 verdict-shape" claim that was prose-only without hook implementation — codex GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE caught the policy/enforcement drift: rule text claimed automated BLOCKING but no T3/T4/T6 hook, verdict parser, schema, or test changed. Per closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md §Outcome A monotone-decline path, downgraded prose closes the drift while the enforcement-hook ship is queued separately)*: when a TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL cite is shipped, the operator MUST: (i) embed `non-automatable: true` marker inline; (ii) embed explicit "no automated enforcement, human gate only" disclosure in commit body acknowledging that automated re-verification is impossible AND naming both verifier-identities (per amendment b); (iii) accept residual provenance gap explicitly. The discipline is OPERATOR-MANUAL — NO T3/T4/T6 hook currently parses `non-automatable: true` as BLOCKING. Promotion to mechanical BLOCKING is FORWARD-REF for a separate enforcement-hook ship: `tools/non_automatable_blocking_audit.py` parsing committed rule edits + emitting BLOCKING status in T3/T4/T6 verdict-shape + regression tests at `tests/test_non_automatable_blocking_audit.py`. Do NOT claim BLOCKING semantic in commit bodies until that ship lands. Forward-only convention codified Wave 18 PSW arc — Bundle B Round-2 commit `d6acfb1`, Bundle B Round-3 commit `217430f`, karpathy 5-author commit `0fe6282`, citation-discipline Round-2 DEMOTE commit `1b87a6e`, citation-discipline Round-3 hard-gate commit `d07ac71`, this Round-4 prose-downgrade commit closes the architectural concern per `reference_wave18_psw_arc_close_4ship_2026_05_04.md` + Outcome A monotone-decline path per `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` (R1 conf=0.86 → R2 conf=0.88 ESCALATING → R3 conf=0.82 DECLINING → R4 conf=0.82 SAME-CLASS DECLINING-STABLE; severity stable medium; severity-gate satisfied via concrete-verification mechanism per amendments a+b + prose-downgrade per amendment c; arc converging within Round 5 ceiling).
34: 
35: 7. **Anthropic-bundled-skill cites: cite the current slash command, not local folder names** — *(specialization of rule #4 for bundled-skill identifier divergence)* — Bundled skill identifiers can diverge across local folders, CCBP tables, changelogs, and official docs. Example: CCBP `changelog/best-practice/concepts/changelog.md:410 @ 76599cb` records a stale-name correction from `/fewer-permission-prompts` to `/less-permission-prompts`, while CCBP `best-practice/claude-skills.md:48 @ 76599cb` still lists the folder-style `fewer-permission-prompts`, AND the current official Commands page (`code.claude.com/docs/en/commands` line 90 [VERIFIED 2026-05-01 via codex T1 web-fetch]) shows `/fewer-permission-prompts` — so the three authoritative surfaces actively disagree. **Discipline**: when sss documents an Anthropic-bundled skill invocation, cite the current command from `code.claude.com/docs/en/commands` FIRST as the tie-breaker; use `code.claude.com/docs/en/skills` only for general skill mechanics; use CCBP tables/changelogs as secondary drift evidence when they conflict. Never treat `.claude/skills/<folder>` or plugin cache paths as proof of the user-visible slash command. Apply to: `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/agents/*.md`, `.claude/commands/**`, `.claude/skills/**/SKILL.md`, `.claude/rules/**`, `docs/sota-grand-catalog/**`, active adoption/citation docs, and commit bodies citing skill invocations. n=1 per `codification-threshold.md`: observed live conflict across official Commands docs / official Skills docs / CCBP changelog / CCBP skills table / sss docs costs ~15 min to re-derive per stale cite, this rule is <200 LOC; re-evaluate at the next Anthropic bundled-skill rename or official/CCBP conflict.
36: 
37: 8. **Composed-claims discipline + mechanical source-class reduction lattice** — *(Wave 17 fire-31 v1 codification per codex T1 fire-28 conf=0.87; UPGRADED at fire-36 per codex T1 fire-33 NEEDS-REVISION on T2 b8fa7dd1 finding "weakest-source rule not computable" conf=0.72)* — When a single normative claim cites MULTIPLE source classes, the cite MUST declare:
38:    - `constituents=[<class1@cite1>, <class2@cite2>, ...]` — explicit list of each constituent primitive at file:line + SHA OR official-docs URL
39:    - `effective_tier=<class>` — the EFFECTIVE class of the composed claim, computed by the reduction lattice below
40: 
41:    **Source-class precedence/order (lower wins; weakest dominates)**:
42:    1. `UNKNOWN` (no cite at all) — always disqualifying; reject claim until cited
43:    2. `TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` (any local glue / sss-novel composition over TIER-1/TIER-2 substrates)
44:    3. `TIER-3-LOCAL-OPERATOR-DERIVED` (sss dogfood, codex verdicts, incidents, operator decisions)
45:    4. `TIER-3-LOCAL-CONFIG` (sss live config or script wiring)
46:    5. `TIER-2` (user-curated/sibling docs, NOT upstream authority)
47:    6. `TIER-1-COMPOSED` (ALL constituents TIER-1 with disclosed local glue) — still TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION as effective tier
48:    7. `TIER-1-DIRECT` (upstream verbatim file:line + SHA OR official-docs URL with anchor)
49: 
50:    **Reduction rule (mechanical)**: `effective_tier = MIN_PRECEDENCE(constituents)`. Concretely: if ANY constituent contains "TIER-3-LOCAL-*" OR the composition glue itself is local-codified (e.g., sss-novel composition pattern), the effective tier is `TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` regardless of how many TIER-1 primitives are cited. EXCEPTION: if the composed claim is ONLY about an upstream primitive's EXISTENCE (e.g., "asyncRewake field exists" cited verbatim from Anthropic docs), it remains `TIER-1-DIRECT` because there's no local composition glue.
51: 
52:    **Anti-promotion rule**: composition CANNOT promote a TIER-2/TIER-3 component into TIER-1. The cite class of the COMPOSED claim is determined by the MIN_PRECEDENCE constituent OR by the composition-glue class, whichever is weaker.
53: 
54:    **Exemplar (computable)**: `.claude/rules/cross-model-consensus.md §Normative Authority Map` row "T1-T7 touchpoint lifecycle":
55:    - `constituents=[TIER-1-DIRECT @ Anthropic-CC-hooks-docs-L1021-1023, TIER-2 @ CCBP-cross-model-workflow.md:1-48, TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION @ sss-novel T0+T7+profile-selection]`
56:    - `effective_tier=TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` (MIN_PRECEDENCE = 2 due to sss-novel glue)
57:    - Despite Anthropic CC + CCBP being upstream-authority for primitives, the composition itself is sss-local and EFFECTIVE class is TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION
58: 
59:    Apply to: any rule body section in `.claude/rules/**` that mixes upstream + sister-rule + local-operator-codification claims. Sister: `cross-model-consensus.md §Normative Authority Map` (POINTS TO this rule, does NOT carry softer duplicate).
60: 
61: ## Anti-patterns (model-name gotchas)
62: - "Plus" in model name does NOT mean quality (qwen3.5-plus = thinking model)
63: - `@latest` on npm may hit Windows cleanup bug → install succeeds but arborist crashes on EPERM (parent `project_gitnexus_full_audit_2026_04_18.md` — PARENT-ATTRIBUTION); prefer `npm install -g` for CLI-bundled MCP servers

Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules\citation-discipline.md:33:6. **TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE class for inherited patterns** — when a SOTA pattern is derived from a named author's published work (book / peer-reviewed paper / conference talk), cite verbatim-quote + author-name + publication inline. Per `.claude/projects/Z--claude-sota/memory/reference_mattpocock_skills_pattern_extract_2026_04_30.md` for the full mining trace + adoption-candidate ranking. **TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE PROVISIONAL-EXCEPTION** *(round-3 framing per T3 round-2 conf=0.88 ESCALATING finding on commit `d4df352`; supersedes round-1 "full cardinal-rule-1 satisfaction" framing — physical-only sources do NOT fully satisfy committed-tree inspectability)*: physical-only printed-book/journal/conference-publication primary anchor (ISBN + page + verbatim-quote) is a PROVISIONAL-EXCEPTION to cardinal-rule-1 (CLAUDE.md L7), NOT full satisfaction. Cite class downgraded to TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL with REQUIRED metadata: (a) ISBN/DOI + page + verbatim-quote inline; (b) verifier-identity disclosure (who-verified-when, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-04 by orchestrator against book Tip #15 verbatim text]`); (c) explicit `non-automatable: true` marker so review hooks know to defer to human sign-off; (d) **HARD-GATE ELIGIBILITY TEST** *(Round-3 amendment a per T3 R3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 same-class finding on commit `1b87a6e`; supersedes round-2 "MUST prefer" soft preference framing — preference is not gateable; eligibility is)*: PROVISIONAL admission is admissible ONLY when DOI landing page + accessible PDF / publisher excerpt / archived scan (license-permitting) was SEARCHED AND UNAVAILABLE. The commit body MUST document the search (which DOI/publisher/archive was queried + return shape — e.g., "searched ACM DOI 10.1145/<doi> 2026-05-05 — paywall-only, no excerpt found; searched Internet Archive — no scan; searched publisher excerpt page — none"). Mere preference is INSUFFICIENT — if any auditable surrogate exists (DOI/PDF/excerpt/scan), use it as the normative source instead of the physical-only book cite. e.g., ACM DOI `https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/<doi>` for ACM papers IS auditable when paywall-free. Secondary aggregator URLs (Wikipedia, `https://pragprog.com/tips/`, `https://martinfowler.com/bliki/<topic>.html`, `c2.com/cgi/wiki`) are VERIFICATION PROXIES only — they help re-verify the primary quote without owning the book physically, but are NOT cited as normative source. **Independent named-reviewer sign-off mandate** *(Round-3 amendment b per T3 R3 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 same-class finding on commit `1b87a6e`; supersedes round-2 "Lower-confidence reviewer-sign-off mandate" sole-orchestrator framing — sole-orchestrator verification is no longer admissible)*: rule edits citing TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL MUST cite a SECOND verifier-identity BEYOND the orchestrator who replayed the verbatim quote against the source — commit body MUST embed BOTH verifier-identity-1 (the orchestrator, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-05 by orchestrator against book Tip #15 verbatim text]`) AND verifier-identity-2 (the independent named reviewer, e.g., `[VERIFIED 2026-05-05 by <human-name> against same book + same page + same quote]`) with explicit `[VERIFIED yyyy-mm-dd by <name> against <source>]` markers for each. The 2-verifier requirement closes the "self-attestation only" gap that round-2 left open. **`non-automatable: true` operator-discipline mandate (manual-only; no hook enforcement YET)** *(Round-4 amendment per T3 round-4 NEEDS-ATTENTION conf=0.82 SAME-CLASS finding on commit `d07ac71`; supersedes Round-3 amendment c "BLOCKING in T3/T4/T6 verdict-shape" claim that was prose-only without hook implementation — codex GPT-5.5 BRIDGE-MODE caught the policy/enforcement drift: rule text claimed automated BLOCKING but no T3/T4/T6 hook, verdict parser, schema, or test changed. Per closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md §Outcome A monotone-decline path, downgraded prose closes the drift while the enforcement-hook ship is queued separately)*: when a TIER-1-NAMED-AUTHOR-QUOTE-PROVISIONAL cite is shipped, the operator MUST: (i) embed `non-automatable: true` marker inline; (ii) embed explicit "no automated enforcement, human gate only" disclosure in commit body acknowledging that automated re-verification is impossible AND naming both verifier-identities (per amendment b); (iii) accept residual provenance gap explicitly. The discipline is OPERATOR-MANUAL — NO T3/T4/T6 hook currently parses `non-automatable: true` as BLOCKING. Promotion to mechanical BLOCKING is FORWARD-REF for a separate enforcement-hook ship: `tools/non_automatable_blocking_audit.py` parsing committed rule edits + emitting BLOCKING status in T3/T4/T6 verdict-shape + regression tests at `tests/test_non_automatable_blocking_audit.py`. Do NOT claim BLOCKING semantic in commit bodies until that ship lands. This rule codifies the 4 disciplines that prevent these drift classes.
4: paths: [".claude/rules/**", ".claude/hooks/scripts/**/*.py", ".claude/agents/**", ".claude/commands/**", ".claude/skills/**/SKILL.md", ".claude/settings.json", "CLAUDE*.md", "*.toml"]

6: 
7: # Port-note discipline — cite stability across port-note insertions
8: 
9: # TIER-3 local codification provenance only: this rule is design-novel for claude-sota (no Z:/claude port source). It codifies 4 cumulative arc patterns surfaced by retrospective T3 audit on 7 commits (37e1710..ba8c6e6) — Pattern P1 port-note 3-line offset propagation (n=4), Pattern P2 FORWARD-REF staleness on downstream materialization (n=2), Pattern P3 inherited upstream cite errors (n=1 strong), Pattern P4 n-counter undercounts (n=2). Verdict file `.claude/state/codex_consult_p4meta_port_note_discipline_OUT.txt` is an evidence trail, not SOTA authority.
10: 
11: A port commit that adds a port-note header of N lines silently shifts every line below it by +N. Cites pointing INTO the ported file from sibling files OR self-cites within the port-note itself use UPSTREAM coordinates by reflex; no compile error fires; readers click and land in the wrong place. n=4 evidence across iter-10/12/13 of this fire's port arc demonstrates the drift class is structural, not coincidental.
12: 
13: This rule codifies 4 disciplines that close the drift classes. Closes 4 cumulative patterns from Agent B retrospective audit per `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md` §Reporting categories T3 axis.
14: 
15: ## §1 The four drift classes
16: 
17: | Class | Symptom | Root cause | n-evidence |
18: |---|---|---|---|
19: | **P1** Port-note line-offset propagation | Cite to `file.py:104` resolves to wrong content | Port-note header insertion shifts all lines below by +N; cite uses upstream coordinates | 4 |
20: | **P2** FORWARD-REF staleness on downstream materialization | Rule says "X is FORWARD-REF; pending P3 port" but X just shipped | No iter-end grep retirement; rule's own update-trigger ignored | 3 |
21: | **P3** Inherited upstream cite errors silently survive verbatim port | Cite to `pyrightconfig.json:134-149 (executionEnvironment)` actually points at experimental-features block | Verbatim port preserves upstream's incorrect parenthetical claim; cite-class audit checks file existence not content | 1 strong |
22: | **P4** Codification undercounts | Commit body claims `n=3` but actual occurrence count is 4+ | Counting from memory not from `grep -c`; promotion gate fires on inflated count | 2 |
23: 
24: ## §2 Discipline 1 — Port-note line-offset compensation
25: 
26: When adding a port-note header of N lines to a ported file, cites pointing INTO that file from anywhere (sibling files, the port-note's own self-cites, README/CLAUDE.md/rules, commit bodies) carry UPSTREAM coordinates by default. Compensate.
27: 
28: **Two valid options. Symbol-anchor preferred per SOTA stability rationale (line numbers volatile, symbols stable):**
29: 
30: 1. **Symbol-anchor (preferred)**: rewrite cites to `file.py:<symbol>` or `file.py:<heading>` or `config.toml:<key>`. Examples:
31:    - `utils.py:104` → `utils.py:log_swallow` (symbol-stable across renames + reorders + line shifts)
32:    - `pyrightconfig.json:156-172` → `pyrightconfig.json:executionEnvironments` (config-key-stable)
33:    - Use line ranges ONLY for exact-text claims where the byte sequence at those lines is the cited content (e.g. SOTA reference quotes that need verbatim line:column).
34: 
35: 2. **Line-bump compensation (fallback)**: when the port edit is a **pure header insertion before the cited span** (no other line additions/deletions), bump every cite by +N where N = port-note line count. Run before commit:
36:    ```bash
37:    N=<port-note line count>
38:    grep -rn "<filename>:[0-9]\+" .claude/ docs/ rules/ commands/ agents/ | \
39:      awk -F: -v n=$N '{ ... bump line numbers by n ... }'
40:    ```
41:    ⚠️ **Caveat**: line-bump is INVALID when the port edit involved any other content addition/deletion below the port-note. In that case, fall back to symbol-anchor or resolve-by-content (`grep <symbol> <ported_file>` to find new line, then update cite).
42: 
43: **Enforcement**: at iter-end, run `grep -nE "<just-ported-filename>:[0-9]+" -r .` and verify each result resolves to expected content. Cite-line grep itself remains iter-end manual (mechanizable as a pre-commit hook in a future iter — FUTURE-AUTOMATION). Post-iter-19 (commit 927532a): T1 codex pre-edit consult IS now hook-driven via `.claude/settings.json` PreToolUse Edit|Write|MultiEdit registration of `codex_t1_consult_gate.py` (sync, no async:true present, timeout 5; default WARN — blocks only under `CODEX_T1_GATE_STRICT=1`). T1 enforcement and cite-line grep are orthogonal disciplines.
44: 
45: ## §3 Discipline 2 — FORWARD-REF retirement audit
46: 
47: Rules + port-notes routinely write `FORWARD-REF — <X> pending P<N> port` for things not yet shipped. When P<N> ships, every such cite must be rewritten to drop the FORWARD-REF qualifier (or escalate to a stronger status — but see §6 anti-pattern about `ACTIVE`).
48: 
49: **Discipline (manual today, mechanizable later)**:
50: 
51: 1. After every ship that lands a previously-FORWARD-REF target, run:
52:    ```bash
53:    # Match BOTH orderings of the FORWARD-REF qualifier
54:    grep -rn "FORWARD-REF.*<filename>" -- .claude/rules/ .claude/hooks/ docs/ commands/ agents/
55:    grep -rn "<filename>.*FORWARD-REF" -- .claude/rules/ .claude/hooks/ docs/ commands/ agents/
56:    ```
57: 2. For each match: rewrite to current status. **DO NOT escalate to ACTIVE without verifying the runtime wire** (see §6 anti-pattern).
58: 
59: **FUTURE-AUTOMATION**: a `codex_postcommit_review.py`-equivalent could enforce cite-retirement at T3. Repo-local `.claude/settings.json:273-335` now has `PostToolUse Bash(git commit *)` registrations, including `codex_postcommit_review.py` plus audit scripts [VERIFIED 2026-04-28], but no dedicated FORWARD-REF retirement grep/audit consumer exists yet. Until that dedicated consumer lands, T3-stage FORWARD-REF retirement remains iter-end grep at manual cadence. T1-stage enforcement and T3 cite-retirement are orthogonal touchpoints.
60: 
61: ## §4 Discipline 3 — Cite-class verbatim verify (recursive synthesis-layer-verify)
62: 
63: Per `.claude/rules/synthesis-layer-verify.md` §Reporting categories, codex T1 verdicts are themselves subject to OVER/UNDER/HONEST-NON-FINDING audit. Pattern P3 (inherited upstream cite errors) demonstrates the recursive application: if a cite is verbatim-ported from upstream BUT the upstream cite was wrong, the port preserves the error AND codex T1 (which checks file:line existence not content) doesn't catch it.
64: 
65: **Discipline**: codex T1 audits on ports MUST resolve every `Reference:` header AND verify the parenthetical claim against actual file content. Add to T1 prompt template:
66: 
67: > For each `Reference: <path>:<line-or-range>` header in the file under review: (a) resolve the cited file:line range and read its content; (b) compare against the parenthetical claim attached to the Reference; (c) flag any mismatch (wrong line range, wrong content type, paraphrased not verbatim).
68: 
69: Scope: applies to `Reference:` headers and `# noqa:` cite annotations and any inline comments of form `(per <file>:<line>)`. Does NOT apply to plain prose mentions of file paths without line cites (those carry no falsifiable claim).
70: 
71: **Inheritance**: when a cite is verbatim-ported from upstream and the parenthetical claim is found WRONG, EITHER fix in the port AND back-port to upstream, OR fix in port + add explicit comment `(corrected from upstream — back-port pending)`. Never silently propagate.
72: 
73: ## §5 Discipline 4 — n-counter audit (claim-vs-actual reconciliation)
74: 
75: When promoting a pattern at n=N codification gate (per `cycle-322 jurisdiction`: n=1 feedback / n=2 rule / n=3 skill or config), verify N matches actual occurrence count BEFORE invoking the promotion.
76: 
77: **Discipline**: every `n=N evidence` claim in commit body / port-note / rule file MUST be backed by an explicit query + scope record:
78: 
79: ```
80: n=4 evidence (verified via `grep -rn '_PROJECT_ROOT' .claude/hooks/scripts/ | wc -l` 2026-04-26):
81:   - utils.py L47 (port _PROJECT_ROOT)
82:   - _guard_base.py L62 (sibling _PROJECT_ROOT)
83:   - shellcheck_guard.py L57 (consumer _PROJECT_ROOT)
84:   - codex_postcommit_review.py:64 (consumer _PROJECT_ROOT)
85: ```
86: 
87: Without the explicit query + scope + line-cited sites, the n-counter claim is inadmissible. Pattern P4 (iter-12 claimed n=3 BLE001; actual n=8) demonstrates that intuition undercounts; greps catch what memory misses.
88: 
89: **Cycle-322 promotion-gate guards**: this rule's existence is itself an n=4-promoted gate (P1) + n=2-promoted gate (P2 + P4). Per discipline §5, those promotions are verified:
90: - P1 n=4: verified via Agent B audit per `agent-a46d7ce915a870480 retrospective verdict §1-§5` — utils.py port-note self-cite L47/L63 + _guard_base.py:6 cite to utils.py:107-134 + pyright_guard.py port-note "utils.py L66" + ruff.toml cite "utils.py:107" = 4 sites.
